Summary by the research lead Not complete yet 01:47

placeholder

  1. SWI/SNF knockouts act alike, and ARID1A and ARID1B loss shifts cells out of the granzyme-high state S13
  2. GATA3 and SWI/SNF set Pdcd1 in opposite directions, so no single chain orders them
  3. INO80C changes a subset of ACTR5's genes in the same direction, apart from SWI/SNF
  4. Each knockout moves only a few percent of cells between named states
  5. PDCD1 loss shifts cells and Tox without lowering its own transcript, and resembles GATA3 loss

Result 1, in the report, 02:08

SWI/SNF knockouts act alike, and ARID1A and ARID1B loss shifts cells out of the granzyme-high state S13

Observation: ARID1A, SMARCC1 and SMARCD2 knockouts change the CD8 T cell transcriptome alike (pairwise r 0.64 to 0.73, against 0.16 for control-guide pairs), ARID1B and ARID2 more weakly, while the ACTR5/INO80C pair forms a separate set.

  • t1 (Whole screen: what each of the 9 chromatin-regulator knockouts (ARID1A, ARID1B, ARID2, ACTR5, INO80C, SMARCC1, SMARCD2, GATA3, PDCD1) changes in tumour-infiltrating CD8 T cells vs CTRL1): explained (Whole-screen question answered by results r1 to r5.)
  • t2 (SWI/SNF knockouts ARID1A, SMARCC1, SMARCD2 (r 0.64-0.73) share one response (up S100a6, Sdcbp2, Casp3, Batf3; down Socs3, Ly6c2; ARID1A also lowers Pdcd1, log2FC -0.47); is it a programme or a shift in state shares?): explained (Answered by t8, t11, t12 results r1 and r5: SWI/SNF set shares a response with a small S13 shift.)
  • beside the threads: finding t14-f2: Where ARID1A's lost S13 share (-0.016, q 0.004) could go: across the 14 states ARID1A cells hold S12 (cycling, Hmgb2/Stmn1) more (+0.013, q 0.007), the only other significant change, and S07 (+0.064, q 0.11), S02 (+0.061, q 0.22) and S10 (-0.054, q 0.10) move by more but not significantly. The S12 gain roughly equals the S13 loss in size, but cells are not followed individually, so a transfer from S13 to S12 is a possibility, not a result; the shift is a small redistribution in a screen where the largest shares move by 0.05 to 0.06 without reaching q<0.05.; finding t15-f1: The 24.5% of cells with several guides are held under their own label (16,946 cells, label 'multi') and are in neither the target nor the control cells of the target-versus-control comparisons; only 16 of ARID1A's 4,622 labelled cells and 22 of PDCD1's 6,279 carry a second guide. Dropping them changes nothing: the S13 share difference from control cells, averaged over units, is ARID1A -0.0123 (negative in 7 of 7 units; all labelled cells -0.0124), ARID1B -0.0100 (6 of 7), ACTR5 -0.0060 (6 of 7), SMARCD2 -0.0035 (5 of 7) and PDCD1 -0.0011 (5 of 7). Averaged over all 7 units, ARID1A and ARID1B lose S13 about two to ten times more than the other three targets.; module E.combinations done (why: multi-guide cells have never been analysed: do paired knockouts add up or buffer?); finding t16-f1: The pair-combination analysis is not testable here: 0 of 0 pairs have both singles with 20 cells in the screen object, which holds the multi-guide cells (16,946) only under one 'multi' label. The multi-guide cells are random co-transductions, not designed pairs: of them 254 carry one target, 2,395 two, and about 14,300 three to ten distinct targets. Of the 2,395 two-target cells most include a CTRL1 guide (e.g. PDCD1+CTRL1 358, ARID1A+CTRL1 337); non-control pairs are small (ARID1A+PDCD1 67, ARID1A+SMARCC1 49, ACTR5+PDCD1 50 cells), and only 25 of 44 two-target combinations reach 20 cells. This is too few for a gene-level test of whether two knockouts add or buffer, so no combination claim is made. (and 1 more)

Result 1, in the report, 02:08, version 6

SWI/SNF knockouts act alike, and ARID1A and ARID1B loss shifts cells out of the granzyme-high state S13

Observation: ARID1A, SMARCC1 and SMARCD2 knockouts change the CD8 T cell transcriptome alike (pairwise r 0.64 to 0.73, against 0.16 for control-guide pairs), ARID1B and ARID2 more weakly, while the ACTR5/INO80C pair forms a separate set1. Guides of one target agree (median r 0.39 against 0.093 for control-guide pairs) 20261113T002825-F-guides_agree-d6b384. All 9 targets differ from the other targets' cells, so part of this similarity may be a response shared by every cutting guide 20261113T014534-D-common_response-5be354. At target level the granzyme-high state S13 falls only for ARID1A (q 0.004) and ARID1B (q 0.004); SMARCD2 does not change (q 0.79), and every perturbed target, PDCD1 and ACTR5 included, sits below the control share4. Averaged over units, the S13 share difference from control is -0.0123 for ARID1A (negative in 7 of 7 units), -0.0100 for ARID1B (6 of 7), -0.0060 for ACTR5, -0.0035 for SMARCD2 and -0.0011 for PDCD1; cells with several guides are held apart under their own label and do not drive this7. In experiment 2 the other targets also fall, but in experiment 1 PDCD1 and SMARCD2 are positive while ARID1A is negative in all three units6. The sign of the S13 loss holds for all 20 SWI/SNF guides, but per-guide significance is mostly ARID1A, and it does not follow own-gene change (Spearman 0.03)5. Inside a state few genes change: 1 to 3 in S13 and 23 for ARID1A in S12, against 1701 genes over all cells for ARID1A3.

Inference: the similarity of the SWI/SNF set is real beyond control guides, and the S13 shift is established for ARID1A, with ARID1B in the same direction; it is partly a change in state shares7.

Limits: S13 was one of 70 target-by-state pairs; the states tool could not compare against other perturbed cells directly; experiment 1 units hold few cells; only 2 control-guide groups calibrate2.

Result 2, in the report, 01:14, version 1

GATA3 and SWI/SNF set Pdcd1 in opposite directions, so no single chain orders them

Observation: against control guides, GATA3 knockout raises Pdcd1 (+0.26, q 4e-4)1. ARID1A (-0.47, q 3e-8), ARID2 (-0.28, q 3e-6), SMARCC1 (-0.17, q 0.01) and SMARCD2 (-0.20, q 0.04) lower it2. The figure shows the same sign inside nearly every state run0099-b225ae. Each of the four SWI/SNF effects is supported by at least 2 of 4 guides alone, and the control-guide groups did not call Pdcd1. For SMARCC1 the DESeq2 pseudobulk test is not significant (p 0.22), so that edge is the weakest 20261113T004231-checks-ce7d14. ARID2 knockout also raises Gata3 (+0.17, q 0.02), with 2 of 4 guides significant3.

Inference: GATA3 loss raises Pdcd1 while SWI/SNF loss lowers it, so a chain GATA3 to SWI/SNF to Pdcd1 is not supported4. The data fit SWI/SNF acting on Pdcd1 directly, with ARID2 possibly adding a GATA3 route. Pdcd1 transcript is a readout, not exhaustion state.

Independent evidence: none yet. No other readout or literature check was made, and the knockout is not confirmed at the own-gene level for every target.

Result 3, in the report, 02:01, version 5

INO80C changes a subset of ACTR5's genes in the same direction, apart from SWI/SNF

Observation: ACTR5 changes 992 genes and INO80C 84 against control cells; 72 of the 84 are also changed in ACTR5, almost all in the same direction1 run0103-604beb. The effect correlation of r 0.89 is computed on genes chosen because INO80C changes them, so it overstates agreement1. Without selecting on INO80C, in a cell-level table of 6,006 genes tested for both, the effects correlate at r 0.57 over all genes and r 0.75 over the 1,865 genes significant in ACTR5 alone, with 85% in the same direction run0156-c3bcaa. Tox is lower in both, with all 4 guides of each target agreeing in direction4. Rpl18 is lower in both, 4 of 4 guides each3. In state composition, the S05 share is higher in ACTR5 (+0.058, q 0.24) and INO80C (+0.022, q 0.23) cells, in the same direction but not established; the ACTR5 rise sits in the four M4/M5 units and is about zero in mouse1 to mouse3, so a batch effect cannot be ruled out2.

Inference: the INO80C signature is a subset of ACTR5's with a moderate genome-wide correlation, which is consistent with one shared programme that ACTR5 moves more strongly; ACTR5 has more power, so a subset is also what power alone would predict, and it does not prove the two act through one complex1. The pair's profile is unrelated to SWI/SNF in the similarity map, which makes INO80 a second axis apart from SWI/SNF, though that map also holds a response shared by all cutting guides1.

Caveat: ACTR5 cells carry about 9% fewer UMIs than control cells, so part of the ribosomal and mitochondrial signature may be depth rather than biology3.

Independent evidence: none; a literature search found no paper on ACTR5 or INO80C in CD8 T cells1.

Limits: this is a knockout screen, so own-gene knockdown is weak evidence of loss, and only 2 control-guide groups calibrate1.

Result 4, in the report, 01:37, version 1

Each knockout moves only a few percent of cells between named states

Observation: 9 of 70 target by state pairs change at q < 0.05, against 0 of 28 for control-guide groups5. PDCD1 cells fill S05 less (3.5% vs 7.6%, q 0.0018)1. SMARCD2 cells fill S07 more (9.6% vs 6.5%, q 0.0037)2. ARID1A cells fill S12 more (3.1% vs 1.8%)3 and S13 less (0.95% vs 2.6%)4. A granzyme/effector score does not fall in step with the S13 loss: ARID1A raises it (+0.018, q 0.019)6.

Inference: the shifts are composition changes of a few percentage points, not population-wide loss of the granzyme programme6. State names rest on markers, and S07 and S08 are unclear5.

Independent evidence: none; no literature checked6.

Result 5, in the report, 01:37, version 1

PDCD1 loss shifts cells and Tox without lowering its own transcript, and resembles GATA3 loss

Observation: PDCD1 cells show Tox up (effect 1.5, q 9.4e-11), with 4 of 4 guides agreeing2. PDCD1's own transcript is not reduced by any of its four guides, so there is no mRNA-level confirmation of the knockout1. Mixscape calls 36% of PDCD1 cells perturbed (2256 of 6279) and none of the control cells, but all those calls sit in the M4 and M5 units4. S04 share is higher in PDCD1 cells within each M unit3. Within states, PDCD1 raises Tox and lowers Ly6c2 and Il7r in S01 and S04, and GATA3 moves the same genes in the same direction with smaller reach; the four-gene vector correlation is r 0.95 in S016. Pdcd1 itself changes sign across states in PDCD1 cells, so that readout is unreliable5.

Inference: the response is not a unit confound, because it holds within units, but it cannot be read as a verified knockout4.

Independent evidence: none; no second readout or literature was checked4.

Finding t16-f1, description, recorded 02:09

The pair-combination analysis is not testable here

The pair-combination analysis is not testable here: 0 of 0 pairs have both singles with 20 cells in the screen object, which holds the multi-guide cells (16,946) only under one 'multi' label. The multi-guide cells are random co-transductions, not designed pairs: of them 254 carry one target, 2,395 two, and about 14,300 three to ten distinct targets. Of the 2,395 two-target cells most include a CTRL1 guide (e.g. PDCD1+CTRL1 358, ARID1A+CTRL1 337); non-control pairs are small (ARID1A+PDCD1 67, ARID1A+SMARCC1 49, ACTR5+PDCD1 50 cells), and only 25 of 44 two-target combinations reach 20 cells. This is too few for a gene-level test of whether two knockouts add or buffer, so no combination claim is made.

Revision history

The S13 (granzyme-high) loss holds in point estimate for all 20 guides of ARID1A, SMARCC1, SMARCD2, ARID1B and ARID2 (share 0.6-2.2% vs 2.6% in control cells; change -0.003 to -0.019), and in 112 of 130 guide-by-unit pairs (n>=10 cells). Per guide, a Mantel-Haenszel test over the units (control cells of the same unit) gives p<0.05 for 4 of 4 ARID1A guides, 2 of 4 SMARCC1, 1 of 4 ARID1B (-2), 1 of 4 ARID2 (-1) and 0 of 4 SMARCD2; the record's propeller guide checks (ARID1A 4/4, SMARCC1 3/4, ARID2 3/4, ARID1B 1/4 beyond the lead test) are the more permissive test, so per-guide significance is partial, while the sign is consistent. (01:40 · reason: Own check of the weakest result against the other targets' S13 shares; narrows 'SWI/SNF set' to ARID1A and ARID1B.)

No checks recordedRuns 1Concerns 1: noted as a limitation

Concerns

The null for the state shifts (r5: "9 of 70 pairs against 0 of 28 control-group pairs") rests on only 2 control-guide groups x 14 states. That is too few to calibrate the false-discovery rate or the control-guide spread. The control guides share cells (CTRL1-1 and CTRL1-11 share 28%) so they are not independent replicates, and the design flags that guides on one vector must be proposed as vectors. Only 7 samples (3 from experiment 1 with few cells) feed propeller. The title "only a few percent" also depends on the 14-state resolution chosen (resolution 1), and is not tested at another resolution. (sk-t11-calib, 01:45)
Would change the conclusion: Draw many random control-guide groups (not 2, no shared cells) with the same cell counts, rerun propeller and take the 95th percentile of the effect and the share of discoveries. Repeat at resolution 0.5 and 1.5. If control groups reach the target effects, or the shifts change with the resolution, the state-shift findings become tentative.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: the design cannot answer itOnly 2 control-guide groups (sharing cells) calibrate, one clustering resolution used; the 9 of 70 pairs at q<0.05 against 0 of 28 control pairs is a weak calibration, stated as such.

The ACTR5/INO80C TNFA signal does not survive the S05 check, and depth is not the reason

The ACTR5/INO80C TNFA signal does not survive the S05 check, and depth is not the reason. In S05 cells, median UMIs are 4,527 for ACTR5 (593 cells), 4,541 for INO80C (220) and 4,552 for controls (1,398), so the UMI depth is matched. With log UMIs and unit as covariates, the ACTR5 TNFA score is -0.0068 against control (p 0.12); UMI-matched within unit it is -0.0060; INO80C is -0.0055 (p 0.39) with UMI adjustment and -0.0082 UMI-matched. So inside S05 ACTR5 does not raise TNFA; the +0.012 of t5-f4 is not seen in S05 cells alone, and the UMI adjustment does not change it.

At gene level the ACTR5 and SWI/SNF effect vectors do not show a reliable opposition

At gene level the ACTR5 and SWI/SNF effect vectors do not show a reliable opposition. Over the 125 TNFA hallmark genes measured, ACTR5 against the mean SWI/SNF vector gives Pearson -0.44 but Spearman -0.09; over all 7,723 expressed genes it gives Pearson -0.07. The control-guide groups, each against the other controls within unit, give correlations of the same size with the SWI/SNF mean over the TNFA genes (Pearson -0.43 to +0.31, Spearman -0.23 to +0.35), so the gene-level correlation is inside the null range. The opposition holds at programme level (t10-f1), not in the gene-by-gene pattern.

Finding t3-f6, description, recorded 01:19

Mixscape calls 36% of PDCD1 cells perturbed

Mixscape calls 36% of PDCD1 cells perturbed (2256 of 6279) and none of the control-guide cells, so the response is carried by a called subset; but the calls are unit-bound: none of PDCD1's cells in the three mouse units are called perturbed (all 2256 are in M4/M5), and 46% of them sit in M5R. In the perturbed cells the within-unit response is larger (181 consistent genes, Pdcd1 up +0.13 to +0.28 in M4 and M5L) and the not-perturbed cells keep a weaker one (14 genes; S04 still 12-23% against 6-10%). The escape split does not remove the response, and it cannot be read as a knockout's own mRNA loss, since Pdcd1 is not reduced in either. ARID1A-PDCD1 similarity (r 0.16 within unit, 0.37 in one batch and 0.13 in the other) is not robust; PDCD1-GATA3 (r 0.39 in both batches, 0 of 66 control pairs as high) is.

No checks recordedRuns 4Concerns 1: noted as a limitation

Concerns

The PDCD1 response (r4) has no confirmation of the knockout: Pdcd1 is not lowered by any of 4 guides (+0.15, +0.12, +0.10, -0.12). Mixscape calls exist only in M4/M5 (2256 of 2256, 46% in M5R); none in mice 1-3, so the "perturbed" label tracks the batch/unit (expt2), and 60% of PDCD1 cells sit in M5R/M5L against 34% of controls. Within-unit tests help, but the response is compared only to control guides: a response shared by cells with any targeting guide (GATA3 r 0.39 with PDCD1 is exactly such a similarity; the survey puts 7 targets in one alike set) would give the same Tox-up/Il7r-down signal. Tox up +1.5 and "resembles GATA3 loss" are therefore not shown to be PDCD1 specific. The result title says "PDCD1 loss", which is a causal reading with no mRNA or protein confirmation. (sk-t3f6-unit, 01:45)
Would change the conclusion: PDCD1 vs all other perturbed cells (reference other_targets, same units, M4/M5 only), and the same per guide in M4/M5 only. If Tox up and the GATA3-like profile vanish against other targets, it is a common response; if they hold, the finding stands with "no knockout confirmation" in the wording. Repeating without M5R (the 46%) would show if one unit carries it.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: the design cannot answer itPDCD1 cells skew to M5R/M5L and the Mixscape calls sit in M4/M5; the PDCD1 response was read within unit, but the design cannot separate target from tumour of origin further.

Between-state composition (propeller over 7 units)

Between-state composition (propeller over 7 units): the share of state S05 is higher in ACTR5 cells (+0.058, q 0.24) and INO80C cells (+0.022, q 0.23) than in control cells, same direction for both but not established at q<0.05. The ACTR5 rise sits in the four M4/M5 units (+0.041 to +0.116) and is about zero in mouse1 to mouse3, so a batch effect cannot be separated from a real one. The S05 hypothesis was picked after the look, and the 0 of 28 control-group null rests on 2 control-guide groups.

No checks recordedRuns 1Concerns 2: 1 left unchecked for time or cost, 1 narrows the findingRevised 1×

Concerns

"Not established" hides that the S05 rise sits in only 4 of 7 units (run 60da3e by_unit). ACTR5 minus control S05 share is +0.060 (M4L), +0.041 (M4R), +0.102 (M5L), +0.116 (M5R), but -0.004 (mouse1), +0.001 (mouse2), 0 (mouse3). INO80C shows the same split: +0.003, +0.018, +0.044, +0.053 against -0.004, +0.001, +0.022. The four M4/M5 units hold about 3,500 of ACTR5's 4,402 cells, and all four go the same way. This is either a lane or batch effect (M4/M5 against mouse1-3) or a real effect that propeller's 7-unit test cannot resolve, since the mouse1-3 units have 61-662 cells. In both cases q 0.24 and 0.23 read as "no shift" when they read as "unresolved". ACTR5 is also beyond the control-group 95th-percentile effect in all 14 states (24 of 28 pairs beyond in this run), so a broad redistribution is not excluded. (sk-t4f2-heterogeneity, 02:00)
Would change the conclusion: Run the test within M4/M5 only and within mouse1-3 only, and check whether control cells differ in S05 share between the two sets of units. A consistent significant rise within M4/M5, with controls stable between them, makes the finding "S05 rise, sample-set specific". No signal in either set supports "not established". The wording should also say "unresolved at 7 units" and not "no shift".
02:00
Left unchecked for time or costThe ACTR5 S05 rise is confined to the four M4/M5 units (+0.041 to +0.116) and is about zero in mouse1-3. It could be a batch or lane effect or a real effect 7 units cannot resolve; I did not run the split test. r3 says the shift is not established and does not claim an effect or its absence.
The finding is labelled a "within-state S05 share", but the run (60da3e) is a between-state composition test (propeller on state proportions). It does not test within-state change. Three further points: (1) "0 of 28 control group x state pairs" rests on only 2 control-guide groups, the weak null already doubted in sk-t11-calib. (2) The q values depend on the number of tests. Here 28 are corrected together, and in the 5-target run (1b6d86) 70 are corrected together with ACTR5 again not changed. A single S05 hypothesis, which this reading singles out after looking, has p 0.103 (ACTR5) and 0.065 (INO80C) uncorrected, so the direction (ACTR5 > INO80C, +0.058 vs +0.022, in line with the earlier weaker-ACTR5 reading) is suggestive but untested. (3) S05 is named only by 4 markers (Ccl5, Lgals3, S1pr1, Actg1) and sets from lung CD8 T cell and DESCARTES megakaryocyte, so "S05" carries no biological meaning here. (sk-t4f2-wording, 02:00)
Would change the conclusion: If the wording becomes "between-state composition, not established at q<0.05; uncorrected p 0.10 and 0.065, same direction for both targets" and the label "within-state" is dropped, the doubt is resolved. A pre-specified S05-only test with each guide alone would show whether the direction is guide-consistent.

Finding t4-f1, description, recorded 01:10, reworded 01:58

INO80C is largely a weaker ACTR5: 72 of INO80C's 84 changed genes

INO80C is largely a weaker ACTR5: 72 of INO80C's 84 changed genes (vs control cells, edgeR) are also changed in ACTR5 (992 genes), 98.6% in the same direction. The r of 0.89 is on genes chosen as significant in INO80C and is inflated. In a cell-level Wilcoxon table of 6,006 genes tested for both, the ACTR5 and INO80C effects correlate at r 0.57 over all genes (Spearman 0.50), r 0.75 over the 1,865 genes significant in ACTR5 alone (85% same sign), and r 0.89 over the 300 most significant ACTR5 genes. The shared set is cytotoxic-effector (Ccl5, Gzma, Gzmc, Ctla4, Eomes, Ctsw, Cd8b1 up; Tox, Tnfrsf4, Ifi27 down) plus ribosomal proteins; ACTR5's greater power also predicts a subset.

No checks recordedRuns 2Concerns 1: noted as a limitationRevised 1×

Context and references

Prior knowledge: No paper returned on ACTR5 or INO80C knockout in CD8 T cells; INO80 complex membership of ACTR5 and INO80C is general prior knowledge from memory, not looked up. (search: ACTR5 INO80 CD8 T cell exhaustion)

Differs from the paper: the similarity result read r 0.62 as two targets alike; the gene overlap shows INO80C's signature is a subset of ACTR5's

Concerns

"INO80C is a weaker ACTR5" and "INO80C's signature is a subset of ACTR5's" compare 84 changed genes (2241 cells) with 992 (4402 cells). Unequal cell numbers (and one ACTR5 guide, ACTR5-1, with 2819 of 4402 cells and own-gene -0.12) drive the gene counts. A subset relation and a weaker magnitude are expected from power alone. The record gives r 0.89 on the 72 shared genes, but those genes are selected as significant in INO80C (selection bias toward concordance). The shared set is also ribosomal and mitochondrial genes with ACTR5 at about 9% fewer UMIs, so shared depth or quality signal is a rival (t10-f3 tests the S05 TNFA score only). The findings and the result also say "knockdown" for a knockout screen. The "no similarity to SWI/SNF" claim has no check beside the 2 control groups. (sk-t4f1-power, 01:44)
Would change the conclusion: Test ACTR5 with its cells thinned to INO80C's number (and to its depth with pt.match_depth), and correlate all-gene effect vectors (not only INO80C-selected genes). If ACTR5 then still changes many more genes with larger effects on INO80C's genes, "weaker ACTR5" stands. If the ACTR5 counts fall to INO80C's range, the difference is power. Excluding ribosomal and mitochondrial genes should also be tried.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: the design cannot answer itINO80C as a weaker ACTR5 is partly predicted by power; r 0.89 is on genes selected as significant in INO80C, so inflated; "knockdown" is the wrong word in a knockout screen. Stated as consistent with a shared complex, not proof.

(01:10 · reason: Recomputed the correlation without selecting on INO80C (the skeptic's doubt sk-t4f1-power).)

No checks recordedRuns 1Concerns 1: noted as a limitation

Concerns

State names rest partly on gene-set names from other tissues: the survey's best-fit sets are TABULA_MURIS lung alveolar macrophage for S13, DESCARTES mesenchyme for S02, kidney tubule for S10, and the finding calls S02 "mesenchyme-like" and S05 "CD8 cytotoxic" (S05 also best-fit a megakaryocyte set). S02 (Junb, Tmsb10) is more plausibly a dissociation/immediate-early stress state in tumour T cells, S13 (Gzme/Gzmd/Gzmf, Igfbp7) could be a doublet or ambient-RNA state (Igfbp7 is a stromal/endothelial gene at 75/15), and S07/S08 are unnamed. The result r5 and r1 build their story on S13 and the other state names ("granzyme-high effector", "naive/central-memory" for S10 from Ccr7/Emb/Hif1a/Tnfsf11). (sk-t5f7-naming, 01:45)
Would change the conclusion: Per-state doublet score and total UMI/ambient-gene check for S13 and S02, plus cell-type markers (Cd3e, Cd8a, Cd4, Foxp3, Lyz2, Igfbp7/Col1a1 co-expression). If S13 is a doublet/ambient state, the S13 loss is a quality shift. If the cells are clean CD8 T cells, names should still be given by their markers (e.g. "Gzmd/e/f-high"), not by other-tissue sets.

Finding t6-f6, description, recorded 00:42

No linear order GATA3 - SWI/SNF - Pdcd1 is supported

No linear order GATA3 - SWI/SNF - Pdcd1 is supported: GATA3 loss raises Pdcd1 while SWI/SNF loss lowers it, so GATA3 would be a repressor of Pdcd1 and not the upstream activator a chain GATA3 to SWI/SNF to Pdcd1 needs; the only edge that runs through the network is SWI/SNF (ARID2) to Gata3, consistent with ARID2 loss lowering Pdcd1 through GATA3 up (a GATA3 path of roughly one third of the ARID2 effect by linear scaling), with ARID1A and SMARCC1/SMARCD2 lowering Pdcd1 directly.

No checks recordedRuns 4Concerns 1: noted as a limitation

Concerns

The reading goes beyond the data in two places. (1) Opposite signs of Pdcd1 under GATA3 loss (+0.26) and SWI/SNF loss (-0.47 for ARID1A) do not exclude a regulatory chain: GATA3 and SWI/SNF can act on Pdcd1 by different routes. (2) "ARID2 lowers Pdcd1 through GATA3 up (about one third by linear scaling)" and "SWI/SNF acts on Pdcd1 directly" are mediation and mechanism claims for which there is no test (the ARID2 to Gata3 edge is +0.17, with 2 of 4 guides significant, pooled lognorm +0.06). Pdcd1 is a low-count marker of state S01, and in PDCD1's own knockout its sign flips across states (t9-f3, -0.17 in S01 vs +0.56 in S04), so it is an unstable readout. The GATA3 within-state rise is significant only in S01 (t13-f3, S06 and S10 not). All of this is also read against only 2 control-guide groups. The GATA3 own-gene knockdown is not shown. (sk-t6f6-causal, 01:45)
Would change the conclusion: Show that the ARID2 effect on Pdcd1 shrinks in cells with Gata3 matched to control (a within-ARID2 test by Gata3 level), and that GATA3-loss and SWI/SNF-loss Pdcd1 signs hold against other perturbed cells and per guide at units level. Absent that, the claim should be restricted to "opposite signs of Pdcd1 transcript"; the chain and mediation statements should be dropped or marked as hypotheses.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: the design cannot answer itOpposite Pdcd1 signs for GATA3 and SWI/SNF do not exclude a chain; no mediation is tested and none is claimed.

The SWI/SNF response is mostly a change in which states cells occupy

The SWI/SNF response is mostly a change in which states cells occupy, with a small within-state part: against control cells of the same state, only 2 to 23 genes change per target and state (ARID1A in S12 has 23, including the shared genes S100a6, Casp3 and Batf3 up and Gzma and Gzmc down; SMARCC1, ARID1B and ARID2 in S13 have 1 to 3), against hundreds of genes changed over all cells (ARID1A 1701). No checks recordedRuns 3Concerns 1: noted as a limitation

Context and references

Prior knowledge: ARID1A loss alters CD8 T cell effector and exhaustion differentiation (memory, not searched) (prior knowledge, unsearched)

Concerns

"Mostly a change in which states cells occupy" compares 2-23 within-state genes with 1701 whole-screen genes. These are not comparable counts. Within-state tests have far fewer cells per target and state (S13 counts as low as 46 in ARID1B), so low power alone gives few genes. In addition the 14 states were clustered on all cells including the perturbed ones, so a state partly encodes the perturbation response (circular). The claim needs a power-matched comparison, not a gene-count ratio. (sk-f3-power, 01:44)
Would change the conclusion: Compute the within-state effect size (per-state vs all-cell effect vectors, correlation, or variance explained) with the whole-screen test down-sampled to the cells of each state. If the all-cell signature is not recovered by state composition plus within-state shifts, the "mostly composition" wording must go.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: the design cannot answer itThe within/composition split is crude (pooled means, not unit-matched) and the within-state gene count is smaller than the whole-screen one; states were clustered on all cells. Stated as "mostly within state in this crude split" with that limit.

Context and references

Prior knowledge: Commentary on an in vitro CRISPR screen reporting that Arid1a loss lets CD8 T cells keep proliferative and cytotoxic function in vivo; consistent in direction with SWI/SNF loss reshaping cytotoxic states, but a different system and readout. (PMID:35820395)

Concerns

The S13 loss (1% vs 3% of cells) is not tied to knockout. Across the 20 guides the S13 change vs own-gene change has Spearman 0.03 (t12-f2), ARID2-1 with no own-gene change drops as much as ARID2-3, and per-guide Mantel-Haenszel is significant for only 4/4 ARID1A, 2/4 SMARCC1, 1/4 ARID1B, 1/4 ARID2 and 0/4 SMARCD2 (t12-f1). Under knockout the own transcript is weak evidence (PF7), but it leaves the guide-level reality of the S13 shift resting mainly on ARID1A. Also no comparison against other perturbed cells was run, so a state shift common to perturbed cells (or tied to a few units' lanes) is not excluded. The ordering across 5 SWI/SNF targets is a pick from a ranked table of 70 target x state pairs. (sk-f2-dose, 01:44)
Would change the conclusion: Propeller of S13 for ARID1A/SMARCC1/SMARCD2/ARID1B/ARID2 with all other targets as the reference, and by half of the units. If the S13 loss disappears against other perturbed cells or in one half of the units, it is not a SWI/SNF effect. Wording "each lowers" should be limited to ARID1A if it fails.
01:47
Noted as a limitation: no tool here does itThe states tool cannot run against other perturbed cells here. Claim narrowed: S13 loss is sign-consistent in 20 of 20 guides, significant per guide mainly for ARID1A (4/4), partial for others, no dose relation (rho 0.03), one of 70 pairs; not shown against other perturbed cells.

Finding f1, description, recorded 00:37

ARID1A, SMARCC1 and SMARCD2 knockouts change the CD8 T cells' transcriptome alike

ARID1A, SMARCC1 and SMARCD2 knockouts change the CD8 T cells' transcriptome alike (pairwise r 0.64-0.73, against 0.16 for the control-guide pairs' line), with ARID1B and ARID2 more weakly alike; the ACTR5/INO80C (INO80) pair forms a separate set (r 0.62) with no similarity to SWI/SNF. Known SWI/SNF and INO80 complex membership is reproduced from the transcriptome alone.

No checks recordedRuns 2Concerns 1: narrows the finding

Context and references

Prior knowledge: ARID1A, SMARCC1, SMARCD2 are SWI/SNF subunits; ACTR5, INO80C are INO80 subunits (MSigDB GOCC_SWI_SNF_COMPLEX / GOCC_INO80_COMPLEX)

Concerns

f1 has no checks and was never compared with other perturbed cells. The similarity run (26695e) is against control guides on pooled profiles. The survey places 7 targets in one alike set, including GATA3 and PDCD1 (mean r 0.30, 12 of 21 pairs past the control pairs). So the r 0.64-0.73 among ARID1A/SMARCC1/SMARCD2 could be a response shared by cells carrying any cutting guide, as opposed to non-cutting controls, or a lane or unit mix effect. The survey notes D.common_response was not given. The wording "reproduced from the transcriptome alone" and "no similarity to SWI/SNF" for INO80 also rest on a 2-group control-guide line. (sk-f1-common, 01:44)
Would change the conclusion: If each SWI/SNF target's profile against all other perturbed cells (reference other_targets, same units) loses the shared genes or the pairwise r falls to the control-pair range, the finding becomes a common-response effect. If it holds and the profile is also computed by_sample, it stands.
01:47
Narrows the findingThe shared-response run finds all 9 of 9 targets differ from the other targets' cells, so a response common to all knockouts exists in the data. The SWI/SNF similarity is therefore read as co-function plus any shared cutting response; the run does not separate them. The claim is narrowed to "similar to each other beyond the control-guide band", not "specific to SWI/SNF". 20261113T014534-D-common_response-5be354

feats=pd.concat([gf,sf],ignore_index=True)
for s in obs.orig.ident.unique() if False else obs["orig.ident"].unique():
    bc=open(f"{w}/exp/{s}_bc.txt").read().split()
    R=sio.mmread(f"{w}/exp/{s}_rna.mtx").T.tocsr(); G=sio.mmread(f"{w}/exp/{s}_sg.mtx").T.tocsr()
    X=sp.hstack([R,G]).tocsr()
    o=obs.loc[bc]; Gd=G.toarray(); rows=[]
    for i,b in enumerate(bc):
        g=o["guide"].iloc[i]; nz=np.nonzero(Gd[i])[0]
        if g=="zNone-1": continue
        if g=="zMulti-1": fc="|".join(guides[j] for j in nz); nf=len(nz); nu="|".join(str(int(Gd[i,j])) for j in nz)
        else: j=guides.index(g); fc=g; nf=1; nu=str(int(Gd[i,j]))
        rows.append((b,nf,fc,nu))
    calls=pd.DataFrame(rows,columns=["cell_barcode","num_features","feature_call","num_umis"])
    ob=pd.DataFrame({"mouse":o["orig.ident"].str.extract(r"^(V\d+_M\d)")[0].values,"deposit_guide":o["guide"].values,"capture_batch":o["batch"].values,"cluster":o["Cluster2"].astype(str).values},index=bc)
    print(s,write(cell_tables,s,X,feats,pd.Index(bc),calls=calls,obs=ob,made_by=f"run:{run_id}",source_files=[str(deposit)+"/GSE203592_integrated_v2.rds.gz"]))

    Per-unit mean log-expression of each target's own gene in each other target's cells, against control cells of the same unit; sign consistency across units

    present []; Empty DataFrame; Columns: []; Index: []

    Why: Build the targets-on-each-other matrix by unit, to check the base's effects and their consistency

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd
ad = pt.screen().adata
obs = ad.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str)
unit = obs['unit'].astype(str)
targets = ['PDCD1','SMARCC1','ARID1A','ACTR5','ARID2','GATA3','ARID1B','INO80C','SMARCD2']
lg = pt.screen().lognorm
genes_present = [g for g in targets if g in ad.var_names]
print('present', genes_present)
rows=[]
units = sorted(unit.unique())
for T in targets:
    if T not in ad.var_names: continue
    gi = ad.var_names.get_loc(T)
    x = lg[:, gi].toarray().ravel()
    for u in units:
        m_ctrl = (lab=='control') & (unit==u)
        m_t = (lab==T) & (unit==u)
        if m_t.sum()<10 or m_ctrl.sum()<10: 
            continue
        rows.append(dict(gene=T, unit=u, n_t=int(m_t.sum()), n_c=int(m_ctrl.sum()), mean_t=x[m_t.values].mean(), mean_c=x[m_ctrl.values].mean()))
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(df.to_string())

    Why: Gene symbols are mouse case; the first pass found none

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd
ad = pt.screen().adata
obs = ad.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
unit = obs['unit'].astype(str).values
print(obs['label_after_qc'].value_counts().to_dict())
targets = ['Pdcd1','Smarcc1','Arid1a','Actr5','Arid2','Gata3','Arid1b','Ino80c','Smarcd2']
vn = {v.lower(): v for v in ad.var_names}
lg = pt.screen().lognorm
rows=[]
units = sorted(set(unit))
for T in targets:
    key = vn.get(T.lower())
    if key is None: print('missing', T); continue
    x = np.asarray(lg[:, ad.var_names.get_loc(key)].todense()).ravel()
    for u in units:
        mc = (lab=='control') & (unit==u)
        for L in targets:
            mt = (lab==L.upper()) & (unit==u)
            if mt.sum()<10 or mc.sum()<10: continue
            rows.append(dict(gene=T, label=L, unit=u, n_t=int(mt.sum()), n_c=int(mc.sum()), mean_t=x[mt].mean(), mean_c=x[mc].mean()))
df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
df['diff']=df.mean_t-df.mean_c
print(df.groupby(['gene','label'])['diff'].agg(['mean','count', lambda s:(s>0).sum()]).to_string())
pt.save_table("t6__own_gene_unit_diffs", df)

    Why: Tests whether the Pdcd1 change in GATA3, ARID1A, ARID2, SMARCC1, SMARCD2 cells holds inside states

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, os
ad = pt.screen().adata
base = "/data/pertura/workspace/screen_GSE203592-8347957c/design/jobs/20261113T002746-C-states_and_composition-9f25d3"
cs = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(base,'cell_states.tsv'), sep='\t').set_index('barcode')
bc = ad.obs_names.astype(str)
st = pd.Series(cs.reindex(bc)['state'].values, index=range(len(bc)))
lab = ad.obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
unit = ad.obs['unit'].astype(str).values
stv = st.values
vn = {v.lower(): v for v in ad.var_names}
x = np.asarray(pt.screen().lognorm[:, ad.var_names.get_loc(vn['pdcd1'])].todense()).ravel()
rows=[]
for L in ['PDCD1','GATA3','ARID1A','ARID2','SMARCC1','SMARCD2','ACTR5','ARID1B','INO80C']:
    for s in sorted(set(stv[~pd.isna(stv)])):
        for u in sorted(set(unit)):
            mt = (lab==L)&(stv==s)&(unit==u)
            mc = (lab=='control')&(stv==s)&(unit==u)
            if mt.sum()>=10 and mc.sum()>=10:
                rows.append(dict(label=L,state=s,unit=u,n_t=int(mt.sum()),n_c=int(mc.sum()),diff=x[mt].mean()-x[mc].mean()))
df=pd.DataFrame(rows)
summ = df.groupby(['label','state']).agg(n_units=('diff','size'), mean_diff=('diff','mean'), pos_units=('diff', lambda s:(s>0).sum()), n_t=('n_t','sum'), n_c=('n_c','sum')).reset_index()
print(summ.to_string())
pt.save_table("t6__pdcd1_within_state", summ)

    Why: Check that each other-target effect holds for each guide, not only the pooled target

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd
ad = pt.screen().adata
lab = ad.obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
guides = ad.obs['guides'].astype(str).values
unit = ad.obs['unit'].astype(str).values
vn = {v.lower(): v for v in ad.var_names}
def col(g): return np.asarray(pt.screen().lognorm[:, ad.var_names.get_loc(vn[g.lower()])].todense()).ravel()
xp, xg, xa, xr = col('Pdcd1'), col('Gata3'), col('Arid2'), col('Arid1a')
mc = (lab=='control')
rows=[]
for L in ['GATA3','ARID1A','ARID2','SMARCC1','SMARCD2']:
    for g in sorted(set(guides[lab==L])):
        m = (lab==L)&(guides==g)
        if m.sum()<10: continue
        rows.append(dict(label=L, guide=g, n=int(m.sum()),
            d_Pdcd1=xp[m].mean()-xp[mc].mean(), d_Gata3=xg[m].mean()-xg[mc].mean(), d_Arid1a=xr[m].mean()-xr[mc].mean(), d_Arid2=xa[m].mean()-xa[mc].mean()))
df=pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(df.round(3).to_string())
print('control guides n per guide:', pd.Series(guides[mc]).value_counts().to_dict())
pt.save_table("t6__by_guide_checks", df)

    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
a = pt.screen().adata

ln = pt.screen().lognorm

idx = np.where(obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).isin(['control','PDCD1']).values)[0]
X = ln[idx][:, gi]
X = np.ravel(X.toarray() if sp.issparse(X) else X)
d = pd.DataFrame({'bc': obs.index.values[idx], 'unit': obs['unit'].values[idx], 'label': obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values[idx], 'pdcd1': X})
d['cls'] = d['bc'].map(cc['class'])
d.loc[d.label=='control','cls'] = 'control'

rows=[]
for u,g in d.groupby('unit'):
    c=g[g.cls=='control'].pdcd1
    for k in ['perturbed','not perturbed']:
        p=g[g.cls==k].pdcd1
        rows.append(dict(unit=u, cls=k, n=len(p), n_ctrl=len(c), mean_p=p.mean(), mean_c=c.mean(), diff=p.mean()-c.mean()))

pn = d[(d.label=='PDCD1')&(d.cls=='not perturbed')]

    Why: t3: does a PDCD1 response survive within each unit, and is it the perturbed cells that carry it

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
a = pt.screen().adata
cc = pt.load('20261113T003927-B-escape-67f919:cell_classes').set_index('barcode')
obs = a.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
bc = obs.index.values
units = obs['unit'].astype(str).values
ln = pt.screen().lognorm
keep_units = ['M4L','M4R','M5L','M5R']
sel = np.isin(lab, ['control','PDCD1']) & np.isin(units, keep_units)
idx = np.where(sel)[0]
M = ln[idx]
M = M.tocsr() if sp.issparse(M) else sp.csr_matrix(M)
cls = np.array([('control' if l=='control' else cc['class'].get(b,'NA')) for l,b in zip(lab[idx], bc[idx])])
u = units[idx]
# expressed genes: detected in >=5% of PDCD1 cells or controls
det = np.asarray((M>0).mean(axis=0)).ravel()
genes = np.where(det>=0.05)[0]
Mg = M[:, genes]
res = {}
for name, mask_fn in [('PDCD1_all', lambda l,c: l=='PDCD1'), ('PDCD1_perturbed', lambda l,c: (l=='PDCD1')&(c=='perturbed')), ('PDCD1_notpert', lambda l,c: (l=='PDCD1')&(c=='not perturbed'))]:
    lmask = np.array([mask_fn(l,c) for l,c in zip(lab[idx], cls)])
    per = {}
    for uu in keep_units:
        t = lmask & (u==uu); c = (lab[idx]=='control') & (u==uu)
        if t.sum()<30: continue
        mt = np.asarray(Mg[t].mean(axis=0)).ravel(); mc = np.asarray(Mg[c].mean(axis=0)).ravel()
        per[uu] = mt-mc
    P = pd.DataFrame(per, index=genes)
    cor = P.corr().round(2)
    # genes with same-sign |diff|>0.1 in all units available
    sgn = np.sign(P)
    consist = ((sgn.nunique(axis=1)==1) & (P.abs().min(axis=1)>0.1)).sum()
    print(name, 'n units', P.shape[1], 'n cells', lmask.sum())
    print(cor.to_string())
    print('genes |diff|>0.1 same sign in all units:', int(consist), ' mean |diff| per unit:', P.abs().mean().round(3).to_dict())
    res[name]=P
for k,P in res.items():
    pt.save_table('t3__unit_diff_'+k, P.reset_index().rename(columns={'index':'gene_idx'}))
print('Pdcd1 unit diffs (perturbed):', res['PDCD1_perturbed'].loc[gi].round(3).to_dict() if (gi:=list(a.var_names).index('Pdcd1')) in res['PDCD1_perturbed'].index else 'not in expressed set')

    calibration: control-guide groups pseudo-tested within unit against other controls, same per-unit signature count and Pdcd1 change

    Empty DataFrame; Columns: []; Index: []

    Why: t3: line for the PDCD1 within-unit signature size against control guides

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
a = pt.screen().adata
obs = a.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
units = obs['unit'].astype(str).values
guides = obs['guides'].astype(str).values
ln = pt.screen().lognorm
keep_units = ['M4L','M4R','M5L','M5R']
ctrl_idx = np.where((lab=='control') & np.isin(units, keep_units))[0]
M = ln[ctrl_idx].tocsr()
det = np.asarray((M>0).mean(axis=0)).ravel()
genes = np.where(det>=0.05)[0]
Mg = M[:, genes]
cg = guides[ctrl_idx]; cu = units[ctrl_idx]
gi = list(a.var_names).index('Pdcd1'); pos = np.where(genes==gi)[0]
out=[]
for g in sorted(set(cg)):
    per={}
    for uu in keep_units:
        t = (cg==g)&(cu==uu); c=(cg!=g)&(cu==uu)
        if t.sum()<100: continue
        per[uu]=np.asarray(Mg[t].mean(axis=0)).ravel()-np.asarray(Mg[c].mean(axis=0)).ravel()
    if len(per)<4: continue
    P=pd.DataFrame(per)
    sgn=np.sign(P).sum(axis=1).abs()==4
    cons=int((sgn & (P.abs().min(axis=1)>0.1)).sum())
    out.append(dict(guide=g, n_units=len(per), n_cells=int((cg==g).sum()), consistent_gt01=cons, mean_abs=round(P.abs().mean().mean(),4), pdcd1_diff_mean=round(P.iloc[pos[0]].mean(),3) if len(pos) else None))
R=pd.DataFrame(out); print(R.to_string())
pt.save_table('t3__control_guide_calibration', R)

    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
a = pt.screen().adata
obs = a.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
units = obs['unit'].astype(str).values
cgrp = obs['control_group'].astype(str).values
ln = pt.screen().lognorm
keep_units = ['M4L','M4R','M5L','M5R']
ctrl_idx = np.where((lab=='control') & np.isin(units, keep_units))[0]
M = ln[ctrl_idx].tocsr()
det = np.asarray((M>0).mean(axis=0)).ravel()
genes = np.where(det>=0.05)[0]
Mg = M[:, genes]
cg = cgrp[ctrl_idx]; cu = units[ctrl_idx]
gi = list(a.var_names).index('Pdcd1'); pos = np.where(genes==gi)[0]
out=[]
for g in sorted(set(cg)):
    per={}
    for uu in keep_units:
        t=(cg==g)&(cu==uu); c=(cg!=g)&(cu==uu)
        if t.sum()<30 or c.sum()<30: continue
        per[uu]=np.asarray(Mg[t].mean(axis=0)).ravel()-np.asarray(Mg[c].mean(axis=0)).ravel()
    if len(per)<2: continue
    P=pd.DataFrame(per)
    allsame=np.sign(P).sum(axis=1).abs()==P.shape[1]
    cons=int((allsame & (P.abs().min(axis=1)>0.1)).sum())
    out.append(dict(group=g, units=len(per), n_cells=int((cg==g).sum()), consistent_gt01=cons, mean_abs=round(P.abs().mean().mean(),4), pdcd1_diff=round(float(P.iloc[pos[0]].mean()),3) if len(pos) else None))

    Why: t3: does the PDCD1-GATA3 similarity (r 0.39) survive unit matching; Pdcd1 axis within unit

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
a = pt.screen().adata
obs = a.obs
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str).values
units = obs['unit'].astype(str).values
ln = pt.screen().lognorm
keep_units = ['M4L','M4R','M5L','M5R','mouse1','mouse2','mouse3']
targets = ['PDCD1','GATA3','ARID1A','ARID1B','ARID2','SMARCC1','SMARCD2','ACTR5','INO80C']
sel = np.where(np.isin(units, keep_units) & (np.isin(lab, targets) | (lab=='control')))[0]
M = ln[sel].tocsr()
det = np.asarray((M>0).mean(axis=0)).ravel()
genes = np.where(det>=0.05)[0]
Mg = M[:, genes]
gnames = np.array(a.var_names)[genes]
L = lab[sel]; U = units[sel]
gi = list(a.var_names).index('Pdcd1'); pos = np.where(genes==gi)[0][0]
prof = {}; pdc = {}; ncount={}
for t in targets:
    per=[]; pp={}
    for uu in keep_units:
        tm=(L==t)&(U==uu); cm=(L=='control')&(U==uu)
        if tm.sum()<30 or cm.sum()<30: continue
        d=np.asarray(Mg[tm].mean(axis=0)).ravel()-np.asarray(Mg[cm].mean(axis=0)).ravel()
        per.append((uu,tm.sum(),d)); pp[uu]=round(float(d[pos]),3)
    if not per: continue
    w=np.array([n for _,n,_ in per],float)
    prof[t]=np.average(np.vstack([d for _,_,d in per]),axis=0,weights=w)
    pdc[t]=pp; ncount[t]=int(sum(n for _,n,_ in per))
P=pd.DataFrame(prof, index=gnames)
C=P.corr().round(2)
print(C.to_string())
print('Pdcd1 change per unit (log-norm diff, target vs same-unit control):')
print(pd.DataFrame(pdc).T.to_string())
print('pooled Pdcd1 within-unit weighted:', {t: round(float(P.loc['Pdcd1',t]),3) for t in P.columns if 'Pdcd1' in P.index})
pt.save_table('t3__within_unit_profile_corr', C.reset_index().rename(columns={'index':'target'}))
pt.save_table('t3__within_unit_pdcd1', pd.DataFrame(pdc).T.reset_index().rename(columns={'index':'target'}))

import pandas as pd, numpy as np, os
from scipy import stats
J='/data/pertura/workspace/screen_GSE203592-8347957c/design/jobs/'
g_all=pd.read_csv(J+'20261113T004422-C-genes-c81a23/changed_genes.tsv',sep='\t')
g_s5=pd.read_csv(J+'20261113T005959-C-genes-e532de/changed_genes.tsv',sep='\t')
def shared(cg):
    a=cg[cg.target=='ACTR5']; i=cg[cg.target=='INO80C']
    return sorted(set(a.gene)&set(i.gene))
sa=shared(g_all); ss=shared(g_s5)
print('whole shared',len(sa), sa[:80])
print('S05 shared', ss)
def ribo_mt(genes):
    g=pd.Series(genes)
    return dict(n=len(g), ribo=int(g.str.match(r'^Rp[sl]\d').sum()), mt=int(g.str.startswith('mt-').sum()))
print('whole ACTR5 ribo/mt', ribo_mt(g_all[g_all.target=='ACTR5'].gene))
print('whole INO80C ribo/mt', ribo_mt(g_all[g_all.target=='INO80C'].gene))

    Code
    
import pandas as pd, numpy as np
ad=pt.screen().adata
o=ad.obs.copy()
X=ad.X
genes=ad.var_names
mt=np.asarray(genes.str.startswith('mt-'))
tot=np.asarray(X.sum(axis=1)).ravel()
mtc=np.asarray(X[:,mt].sum(axis=1)).ravel()
o['mtfrac']=mtc/np.maximum(tot,1)
o['tot']=tot
rows=[]
for lab in ['control','ACTR5','INO80C','ARID1A','SMARCC1']:
    s=o[o.label_after_qc==lab]
    rows.append(dict(label=lab,n=len(s),umis_med=s.umis.median(),genes_med=s.genes.median(),mt_med=s.mtfrac.median()))
t=pd.DataFrame(rows); print(t.to_string())
# sample-matched: mean of within-sample differences in umis and mt fraction
res=[]
for lab in ['ACTR5','INO80C']:
    d=[]
    for smp,s in o.groupby('sample'):
        a=s[s.label_after_qc==lab]; c=s[s.label_after_qc=='control']
        if len(a)>=10 and len(c)>=10:
            d.append((np.log10(a.umis.median())-np.log10(c.umis.median()), a.mtfrac.median()-c.mtfrac.median()))
    d=np.array(d)
    res.append((lab,len(d), d[:,0].mean(), d[:,1].mean()))
print(res)
pt.save_table('t4__depth_check', t)

    Why: t10: does the ACTR5/INO80C TNFA signal survive a UMI-depth check at S05

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scanpy as sc
import statsmodels.formula.api as smf
a = pt.screen().adata
tnfa = ["Abca1","Ackr3","Areg","Atf3","Atp2b1","B4galt1","B4galt5","Bcl2a1d","Bcl3","Bcl6","Bhlhe40","Birc2","Birc3","Bmp2","Btg1","Btg2","Btg3","Ccl20","Ccl5","Ccn1","Ccnd1","Ccnl1","Ccrl2","Cd44","Cd69","Cd80","Cd83","Cdkn1a","Cebpb","Cebpd","Cflar","Clcf1","Csf1","Csf2","Cxcl1","Cxcl10","Cxcl11","Cxcl2","Cxcl5","Dennd5a","Dnajb4","Dram1","Dusp1","Dusp2","Dusp4","Dusp5","Edn1","Efna1","Egr1","Egr2","Egr3","Ehd1","Eif1","Ets2","F2rl1","F3","Fjx1","Fos","Fosb","Fosl1","Fosl2","Fut4","G0s2","Gadd45a","Gadd45b","Gch1","Gem","Gfpt2","Gpr183","Hbegf","Hes1","Icam1","Icosl","Id2","Ier2","Ier3","Ier5","Ifih1","Ifit2","Ifngr2","Il12b","Il15ra","Il18","Il1a","Il1b","Il23a","Il6","Il6st","Il7r","Inhba","Irf1","Irs2","Jag1","Jun","Junb","Kdm6b","Klf10","Klf2","Klf4","Klf6","Klf9","Kynu","Lamb3","Ldlr","Lif","Litaf","Maff","Map2k3","Map3k8","Marcks","Mcl1","Msc","Mxd1","Myc","Nampt","Nfat5","Nfe2l2","Nfil3","Nfkb1","Nfkb2","Nfkbia","Nfkbie","Ninj1","Nr4a1","Nr4a2","Nr4a3","Olr1","Panx1","Pde4b","Pdlim5","Per1","Pfkfb3","Phlda1","Phlda2","Plau","Plaur","Plek","Plk2","Plpp3","Pmepa1","Pnrc1","Ppp1r15a","Ptger4","Ptgs2","Ptpre","Ptx3","Rcan1","Rel","Rela","Relb","Rhob","Rigi","Ripk2","Rnf19b","Sat1","Sdc4","Serpinb2","Serpinb8","Serpine1","Sgk1","Slc16a6","Slc2a3","Slc2a6","Smad3","Snn","Socs3","Sod2","Sphk1","Spsb1","Sqstm1","Stat5a","Tank","Tap1","Tgif1","Tiparp","Tlr2","Tnc","Tnf","Tnfaip2","Tnfaip3","Tnfaip6","Tnfaip8","Tnfrsf9","Tnfsf9","Tnip1","Tnip2","Traf1","Trib1","Trip10","Tsc22d1","Tubb2a","Vegfa","Yrdc","Zbtb10","Zc3h12a","Zfp36"]
cells = pt.load_cells("state_S05-aaaa8ea1")
ids = set(cells['barcode'])
o = a.obs
s05 = o[o.index.isin(ids)].copy()
print("S05 cells", len(s05), s05['label_after_qc'].value_counts().to_dict())
# score on the S05 cells, from lognorm
ad = a[s05.index].copy()
sc.pp.normalize_total(ad, target_sum=1e4)
sc.pp.log1p(ad)
genes_ok = [g for g in tnfa if g in ad.var_names]
print("tnfa genes present", len(genes_ok))
sc.tl.score_genes(ad, genes_ok, ctrl_size=50, n_bins=25, random_state=0, score_name="tnfa")
df = pd.DataFrame({"score": ad.obs["tnfa"].values, "label": s05['label_after_qc'].values,
                   "umis": np.log10(s05['umis'].values.astype(float)), "sample": s05['sample'].astype(str).values,
                   "umis_raw": s05['umis'].values}, index=s05.index)
df = df[df.label.isin(["control","ACTR5","INO80C"])].copy()
df["tgt"] = (df.label!="control").astype(int)
print(df.groupby("label").agg(n=("score","size"), med_umis=("umis_raw","median"), mean_score=("score","mean")).to_string())
out = {}
for t in ["ACTR5","INO80C"]:
    d = df[df.label.isin([t,"control"])].copy()
    d["is_t"] = (d.label==t).astype(int)
    m0 = smf.ols("score ~ is_t + C(sample)", data=d).fit()
    m1 = smf.ols("score ~ is_t + umis + C(sample)", data=d).fit()
    # UMI-matched: within sample, bin log-UMI into deciles pooled over both arms, average control-vs-target differences
    d["bin"] = d.groupby("sample")["umis"].transform(lambda x: pd.qcut(x.rank(method="first"), 10, labels=False))
    diffs=[]; wts=[]
    for (smp,b),g in d.groupby(["sample","bin"]):
        nt=(g.is_t==1).sum(); nc=(g.is_t==0).sum()
        if nt>=5 and nc>=5:
            diffs.append(g.loc[g.is_t==1,"score"].mean()-g.loc[g.is_t==0,"score"].mean()); wts.append(nt)
    matched = float(np.average(diffs,weights=wts)) if diffs else float("nan")
    out[t] = dict(n_target=int(d.is_t.sum()), n_control=int((d.is_t==0).sum()),
                  effect_sample_adj=float(m0.params["is_t"]), p_sample_adj=float(m0.pvalues["is_t"]),
                  effect_umi_adj=float(m1.params["is_t"]), p_umi_adj=float(m1.pvalues["is_t"]),
                  umi_coef=float(m1.params["umis"]),
                  effect_umi_matched=matched, bins_used=len(diffs))
    print(t, out[t])
res = pd.DataFrame(out).T
pt.save_table("t10__depth_S05_tnfa", res.reset_index().rename(columns={"index":"target"}))
print(res.to_string())
    Output

sc.pp.log1p(ad)
genes_ok = [g for g in tnfa if g in ad.var_names]
sc.tl.score_genes(ad, genes_ok, ctrl_size=50, n_bins=25, random_state=0, score_name="tnfa")
df = pd.DataFrame({"score": ad.obs["tnfa"].values, "label": s05['label_after_qc'].values,
                   "logumi": np.log10(s05['umis'].values.astype(float)), "unit": s05['unit'].astype(str).values,
                   "umis_raw": s05['umis'].values}, index=s05.index)
df = df[df.label.isin(["control","ACTR5","INO80C"])].copy()
print(df.groupby("label").agg(n=("score","size"), med_umis=("umis_raw","median"), mean_score=("score","mean")).to_string())
out = {}
for t in ["ACTR5","INO80C"]:
    d = df[df.label.isin([t,"control"])].copy()
    d["is_t"] = (d.label==t).astype(int)
    m0 = smf.ols("score ~ is_t + C(unit)", data=d).fit()
    m1 = smf.ols("score ~ is_t + logumi + C(unit)", data=d).fit()
    # within-unit UMI deciles by rank
    d["r"] = d.groupby("unit")["logumi"].rank(method="first")
    d["n_u"] = d.groupby("unit")["logumi"].transform("size")
    d["bin"] = np.floor((d["r"]-1)/d["n_u"]*10).astype(int)
    diffs=[]; wts=[]
    for (u,b),g in d.groupby(["unit","bin"]):
        nt=(g.is_t==1).sum(); nc=(g.is_t==0).sum()
        if nt>=5 and nc>=5:
            diffs.append(g.loc[g.is_t==1,"score"].mean()-g.loc[g.is_t==0,"score"].mean()); wts.append(nt)
    matched = float(np.average(diffs,weights=wts)) if diffs else float("nan")
    out[t] = dict(n_target=int(d.is_t.sum()), n_control=int((d.is_t==0).sum()),
                  effect_unit_adj=float(m0.params["is_t"]), p_unit_adj=float(m0.pvalues["is_t"]),
                  effect_umi_adj=float(m1.params["is_t"]), p_umi_adj=float(m1.pvalues["is_t"]),
                  umi_coef=float(m1.params["logumi"]),
                  effect_umi_matched=matched, bins_used=len(diffs))
    print(t, out[t])
res = pd.DataFrame(out).T.reset_index().rename(columns={"index":"target"})
pt.save_table("t10__depth_S05_tnfa", res)
print(res.to_string())

ctrl_groups = sorted([g for g in set(ctrl_grp) if g.startswith("CTRL")])
units = sorted(set(unit))
ctrl_mask = lab=="control"
# null: each control guide group vs the other controls, same unit
null_rows=[]
for g in ctrl_groups:
    gm = (ctrl_grp==g)&ctrl_mask
    if gm.sum()<50: continue
    rows=[]
    for u in units:
        mt=gm&(unit==u); mc=ctrl_mask&(~(ctrl_grp==g))&(unit==u)
        if mt.sum()>=10 and mc.sum()>=10:
            rows.append(np.asarray(Xg[mt].mean(axis=0)).ravel()-np.asarray(Xg[mc].mean(axis=0)).ravel())
    if not rows: continue
    v=np.mean(rows,axis=0)
    gn = pd.Series(v, index=genes).reindex(EV.index).fillna(0).values
    r_all,_=pearsonr(gn, swi_mean.values); r_tn,_=pearsonr(gn[tn], swi_mean.values[tn])
    rho_tn,_=spearmanr(gn[tn], swi_mean.values[tn])
    null_rows.append(dict(control_group=g, cells=int(gm.sum()), units=len(rows), pearson_all_vs_SWI=r_all, pearson_TNFA_vs_SWI=r_tn, spearman_TNFA_vs_SWI=rho_tn))
N = pd.DataFrame(null_rows)
print(N.round(4).to_string())
pt.save_table("t10__null_corr", N)

import numpy as np, pandas as pd, scipy.sparse as sp
from scipy.stats import spearmanr, pearsonr
a = pt.screen().adata
obs = a.obs.copy()
cs = pt.load('20261113T002746-C-states_and_composition-9f25d3:cell_states').set_index('barcode')
obs['state'] = cs.reindex(obs.index)['state'].values
obs['guide'] = obs['guides'].astype(str).str.split(':').str[0]
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str)
targets = ['ARID1A','SMARCC1','SMARCD2','ARID1B','ARID2','ACTR5','INO80C','GATA3','PDCD1']
# lognorm of raw counts
X = a.X
tot = np.asarray(X.sum(axis=1)).ravel()
D = sp.diags(1e4/np.maximum(tot,1))
L = (D @ X).tocsr(); L.data = np.log1p(L.data)
gcol = {g.upper(): i for i,g in enumerate(a.var_names)}
def gene_vec(sym):
    i = gcol[sym.upper()]
    return np.asarray(L[:, i].todense()).ravel()
own = {t: gene_vec(t) for t in targets}
ctrl = obs[lab=='control']
ctrl_s13 = (ctrl.state=='S13').mean(); ctrl_s07=(ctrl.state=='S07').mean()
print('control S13', ctrl_s13, 'S07', ctrl_s07, 'n', len(ctrl))
rows=[]; unit_rows=[]
ctrl_unit = ctrl.groupby('unit').apply(lambda d: pd.Series({'s13':(d.state=='S13').mean(),'s07':(d.state=='S07').mean(),'n':len(d)}))
for t in targets:
    m = (lab==t)&(obs.guide.str.startswith(t+'-'))
    for g in sorted(obs.loc[m,'guide'].unique()):
        mg = m & (obs.guide==g)
        d = obs[mg]
        ow = own[t][mg.values]; oc = own[t][(lab=='control').values]
        r = dict(target=t, guide=g, n=len(d), s13=(d.state=='S13').mean(), s07=(d.state=='S07').mean(),
                 d_s13=(d.state=='S13').mean()-ctrl_s13, d_s07=(d.state=='S07').mean()-ctrl_s07,
                 own_lfc=ow.mean()-oc.mean(), frac_own_zero=(ow==0).mean())
        # per unit
        ups13=[];ups07=[]
        for u in sorted(d.unit.unique()):
            du = d[d.unit==u]
            if len(du)<10: continue
            cu = ctrl[ctrl.unit==u]
            ds13=(du.state=='S13').mean()-(cu.state=='S13').mean()
            ds07=(du.state=='S07').mean()-(cu.state=='S07').mean()
            unit_rows.append(dict(target=t,guide=g,unit=u,n=len(du),d_s13=ds13,d_s07=ds07))
        rows.append(r)
G = pd.DataFrame(rows)
U = pd.DataFrame(unit_rows)
pd.set_option('display.width',250)
print(G.round(4).to_string())

from scipy.stats import spearmanr, pearsonr
# own-gene log effects of each guide, as the record's guides check reports them (run 20261113T003742-checks-a703d4)

 'SMARCD2-1':-0.37669,'SMARCD2-2':-0.53230,'SMARCD2-3':-1.33767,'SMARCD2-4':-0.68915}

    print(tgt, 'n_guides', len(s), 'S13 down', (s.d_s13<0).sum(), 'S07 up', (s.d_s07>0).sum())

    Why: Per-guide significance for S13 and S07 with units as strata

    Code
    
import numpy as np, pandas as pd
from statsmodels.stats.contingency_tables import StratifiedTable
a = pt.screen().adata
obs = a.obs.copy()
cs = pt.load('20261113T002746-C-states_and_composition-9f25d3:cell_states').set_index('barcode')
obs['state'] = cs.reindex(obs.index)['state'].values
obs['guide'] = obs['guides'].astype(str).str.split(':').str[0]
lab = obs['label_after_qc'].astype(str)
ctrl = obs[lab=='control']
out=[]
for g in sorted([x for x in obs.guide.unique() if x.split('-')[0] in ['ARID1A','SMARCC1','ARID1B','ARID2','SMARCD2'] and x.split('-')[0]==x.split('-')[0]]):
    t = g.split('-')[0]
    gd = obs[(obs.guide==g)&(lab==t)]
    for st_ in ['S13','S07']:
        tabs=[]; 
        for u in sorted(gd.unit.unique()):
            gu = gd[gd.unit==u]; cu = ctrl[ctrl.unit==u]
            if len(gu)<10 or len(cu)<10: continue
            a1=(gu.state==st_).sum(); b1=len(gu)-a1
            a0=(cu.state==st_).sum(); b0=len(cu)-a0
            tabs.append(np.array([[a1,b1],[a0,b0]])+0.5)
        if len(tabs)<2: continue
        T = StratifiedTable(tabs)
        r = T.test_null_odds(); ci = T.oddsratio_pooled_confint()
        out.append(dict(guide=g, state=st_, units=len(tabs), OR=round(T.oddsratio_pooled,3), ci=(round(ci[0],3),round(ci[1],3)), p=float('%.3g'%r.pvalue)))
R = pd.DataFrame(out)
print(R.to_string())
pt.save_table('t12__per_guide_MH', R)

    Why: separates regulation from composition by the standard two-part split

    Code
    import numpy as np, pandas as pd
a = pt.screen().adata
st = pt.load("20261113T002746-C-states_and_composition-9f25d3:cell_states").set_index('barcode')
obs = a.obs.copy()
obs['state'] = st['state'].reindex(obs.index.astype(str)).values if False else None
bc = obs.index.astype(str)
m = st['state'].reindex(bc)
print('matched', m.notna().sum(), 'of', len(bc))
print(obs['label_after_qc'].value_counts().to_dict())
g = list(a.var_names).index('Pdcd1')
L = pt.screen().lognorm
x = np.asarray(L[:, g].todense()).ravel() if hasattr(L,'todense') else np.asarray(L[:, g]).ravel()
df = pd.DataFrame({'label': obs['label_after_qc'].values, 'unit': obs['unit'].values, 'state': m.values, 'pdcd1': x})
df = df[df['label'].isin(['control','GATA3','PDCD1','ARID1A']) & df['state'].notna()]
rows=[]
ctrl = df[df.label=='control']
wC = ctrl.groupby('state').size()/len(ctrl)
muC = ctrl.groupby('state')['pdcd1'].mean()
for t in ['GATA3','PDCD1','ARID1A']:
    d = df[df.label==t]
    wT = d.groupby('state').size()/len(d)
    muT = d.groupby('state')['pdcd1'].mean()
    states = sorted(set(wT.index)|set(wC.index))
    wT=wT.reindex(states).fillna(0); wC=wC.reindex(states).fillna(0)
    muT=muT.reindex(states); muC=muC.reindex(states)
    within_s = (muT-muC)
    within = float((wT*within_s.fillna(0)).sum())
    comp = float(((wT-wC)*muC.fillna(0)).sum())
    total = float(d.pdcd1.mean()-ctrl.pdcd1.mean())
    rows.append({'target':t,'total_diff':total,'within_state':within,'composition':comp,'sum_check':within+comp})
    for s in ['S05','S06','S10','S01','S04']:
        rows.append({'target':t,'state':s,'share_target':float(wT.get(s,0)),'share_ctrl':float(wC.get(s,0)),'mu_target':float(muT.get(s,np.nan)),'mu_ctrl':float(muC.get(s,np.nan)),'within_diff':float(within_s.get(s,np.nan)),'n_target':int((d.state==s).sum())})
out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
pd.set_option('display.width',250)
print(out.round(4).to_string())
pt.save_table("t13__decomposition", out)

    Why: sk-t4f1-power: selection on ACTR5 alone does not bias INO80C's effects

    Code
    import pandas as pd, numpy as np
from scipy.stats import pearsonr, spearmanr
t = pt.load('genes-8f4d3a14')
a = t[t.target=='ACTR5'].set_index('gene'); i = t[t.target=='INO80C'].set_index('gene')
g = a.index.intersection(i.index)
a, i = a.loc[g], i.loc[g]
out = {'genes_tested_both': len(g)}
out['all_pearson'], out['all_spearman'] = pearsonr(a.effect,i.effect)[0], spearmanr(a.effect,i.effect)[0]
sel = a.q<0.05
out['actr5_sig_n'] = int(sel.sum())
out['actr5_sig_pearson'] = pearsonr(a.effect[sel],i.effect[sel])[0]
out['actr5_sig_spearman'] = spearmanr(a.effect[sel],i.effect[sel])[0]
out['same_sign_frac_actr5_sig'] = float((np.sign(a.effect[sel])==np.sign(i.effect[sel])).mean())
top = a.q.nsmallest(300).index
out['top300_pearson'] = pearsonr(a.effect[top],i.effect[top])[0]
pt.save_table('t4__actr5_ino80c_allgenes', pd.DataFrame([out]))
print(out)

    Why: test whether multi-guide cells drive the S13 loss

    Code
    import pandas as pd, numpy as np
o = pt.screen().adata.obs.copy()
cs = pt.load('20261113T012359-C-states_and_composition-1b6d86:cell_states').set_index('barcode')
o['state']=cs.state.reindex(o.index)
o = o[o.label_after_qc.notna() & (o.label_after_qc!='') & o.state.notna()]
o['multi']=o.guides.str.contains(';',na=False)
print(o.label_after_qc.value_counts().to_dict()); print('multi frac',o.multi.mean().round(3))
rows=[]
ctl = [l for l in o.label_after_qc.unique() if 'CTRL' in str(l).upper() or 'non' in str(l).lower()]
print('controls',ctl)
for tgt in ['ARID1A','ARID1B','SMARCD2','ACTR5','PDCD1']:
    for subset,name in [(o,'all'),(o[~o.multi],'single')]:
        d=[]
        for u,g in subset.groupby('unit'):
            t=g[g.label_after_qc==tgt]; c=g[g.label_after_qc.isin(ctl)]
            if len(t)>=10 and len(c)>=10:
                d.append((t.state.eq('S13').mean()-c.state.eq('S13').mean(), len(t)))
        w=np.array([x[1] for x in d]); v=np.array([x[0] for x in d])
        rows.append(dict(target=tgt,cells=name,units=len(d),neg=int((v<0).sum()),mean_diff=v.mean(),wmean=(v*w).sum()/w.sum(),n_cells=int(w.sum())))
r=pd.DataFrame(rows).round(4); print(r.to_string()); pt.save_table('t15__s13_single_guide',r)

    Recheck S13 difference with correct control label, single-guide cells vs all labelled cells

    multi False True; label_after_qc; ACTR5 4395 7; ARID1A 4606 16; ARID1B 2462 3; ARID2 3663 1; GATA3 2603 3; INO80C 2240 1; PDCD1 ...

    Why: test whether multi-guide cells drive the S13 loss

    Code
    import pandas as pd, numpy as np
o = pt.screen().adata.obs.copy()
cs = pt.load('20261113T012359-C-states_and_composition-1b6d86:cell_states').set_index('barcode')
o['state']=cs.state.reindex(o.index); o=o[o.state.notna()]
o['multi']=o.guides.str.contains(';',na=False)
print(pd.crosstab(o.label_after_qc, o.multi).to_string())
rows=[]
for tgt in ['ARID1A','ARID1B','SMARCD2','ACTR5','PDCD1']:
    for subset,name in [(o,'all'),(o[~o.multi],'single')]:
        d=[]
        for u,g in subset.groupby('unit'):
            t=g[g.label_after_qc==tgt]; c=g[g.label_after_qc=='control']
            if len(t)>=10 and len(c)>=10: d.append((t.state.eq('S13').mean()-c.state.eq('S13').mean(), len(t)))
        w=np.array([x[1] for x in d]); v=np.array([x[0] for x in d])
        rows.append(dict(target=tgt,cells=name,units=len(d),neg=int((v<0).sum()),mean_diff=v.mean(),n_cells=int(w.sum())))
r=pd.DataFrame(rows).round(4); print(r.to_string()); pt.save_table('t15__s13_single_guide',r)

    Why: is a combination analysis feasible at all

    Code
    import pandas as pd, itertools, collections
o = pt.screen().adata.obs
m = o[o.label_after_qc=='multi']
def tg(s):
    return tuple(sorted({x.split(':')[0].rsplit('-',1)[0] for x in s.split(';')}))
t = m.guides.apply(tg)
print(t.apply(len).value_counts().to_dict())
c = t[t.apply(len)==2].value_counts()
print(len(c)); print(c.head(15).to_string())
print('with control member', t.apply(lambda x:'CTRL1' in x).sum())
print('pair >=20 cells', int((c>=20).sum()))

    Generated from the modules

    Analyses

    Each tool the agent calls, with its analyses. The package functions and the example figures come from runs on synthetic screens. Each analysis has its entry in the knowledge base.

    guides

    Guide calls and their evidence, cells per target, each target's own gene, escape (Mixscape or Mixscale), dose, clones, the vectors' structure, and the controls that cut against those that do not

    The evidence behind each cell's guide call · calls

    The evidence for each cell's guide call.

    Applies to: always

    Each target's cells against the control guides' · cells

    Each target's cell count against the control guides'.

    Applies to: always (read against the library's representation when there is one)

    Calls: MAGeCK: test

    Each guide's change of its own target gene · own_gene

    Each guide's change of its own target gene, described against a line.

    Applies to: a modality acting on the target gene (CRISPRi, CRISPRa, knockout)

    Perturbed and non-perturbed cells within a target's cells · escape

    Within a target's cells, some cells were changed by the perturbation and some were not (mosaic editing, in-frame edits, weak knockdown in some cells).

    Applies to: CRISPRi or knockout

    Calls: pertpy: tl.Mixscape.perturbation_signature, tl.Mixscape.mixscape; scanpy: pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.pca

    How much a target's guides differ in efficacy · dose

    A target's guides can differ in efficacy, so pooling them averages over their efficacies (G4, PF8).

    Applies to: CRISPRi or CRISPRa

    Expanded clones among a guide's cells · clones

    A guide's cells can descend from a few transduced cells that expanded.

    Applies to: in vivo

    Guides that travel on one vector · vectors

    Some libraries put a target's guides on one vector (dual-guide vectors, arrays).

    Applies to: vectors of two or more guides, or two guides per cell by design

    The response to cutting DNA · cutting

    The response to cutting itself: the cells of control guides that cut where no gene is (intergenic, safe-targeting) against the non-targeting ones.

    Applies to: control guides that target DNA where no gene is (intergenic, safe-targeting) beside non-targeting ones, under any modality

    impact

    Which targets change the cells, and how much, against the controls; the response many targets share

    Each target's effect on the whole profile · distance

    A target's effect on the cells' whole profile.

    Applies to: always

    Calls: pertpy: tl.DistanceTest; scanpy: pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.pca; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    A target's effect beyond the response all perturbations share · shared

    The part of a target's effect that remains once the response every perturbation shares is set aside.

    Applies to: 3 or more targets

    Calls: pertpy: tl.DistanceTest; scanpy: pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.pca; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    genes

    The genes each target changes; named genes read across the targets (genes); the targets' network (genes targets_own)

    The genes each target changes · genes

    Most published screens test a target's cells against the control cells, most often with a Wilcoxon test; fewer sum the cells by replicate (pseudobulk).

    Applies to: always

    Calls: Seurat: FindMarkers; edgeR: glmQLFit, glmQLFTest; pertura: a rank test within each sample (its own code, until the module is converted); scanpy: tl.rank_genes_groups

    scores

    Each target's effect on per-cell scores: an MSigDB collection (gene_sets, select), lists of your own (gene_lists), the control cells' contrast between two levels (contrast), the proteins (gene_sets proteins), or any per-cell value (cell_values)

    The gene programs each target moves · scores

    Coordinated gene programs, and each target's effect on each.

    Applies to: always

    Calls: cnmf: cNMF prepare, factorize, combine, consensus; scanpy: tl.rank_genes_groups; scanpy: tl.score_genes; scikit-learn: decomposition.NMF; scipy: stats.ranksums; statsmodels: OLS; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    states

    Cell states with their naming evidence and each target's shares of them; changes within a state; the cell cycle; a trajectory (trajectory_root)

    Cell states, and each target's share of them · states

    Cell states found without the perturbation labels (Leiden clusters named by their markers), and each target's share of cells in each state against the control cells.

    Applies to: always

    Calls: scanpy: pp.normalize_total, pp.log1p, pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.scale, tl.pca, pp.neighbors, tl.leiden, tl.umap, tl.rank_genes_groups; scipy: stats.fisher_exact; speckle: getTransformedProps, propeller.ttest; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    Changes within a state, beside shifts between states · within

    It tells a target that moves cells between states from one that changes cells within a state.

    Applies to: a target whose share of a state shifted (C.states_and_composition)

    Calls: scanpy: tl.rank_genes_groups

    Each target's cell-cycle phases · cell_cycle

    Each cell's phase from the S and G2/M genes (Tirosh et al.

    Applies to: human or mouse (experiment.organism)

    Calls: scanpy: tl.score_genes_cell_cycle; scipy: stats.fisher_exact; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    programs

    Gene programmes found in the cells (cNMF, unless choices gene_sets names another method), with each target's usage

    The gene programs each target moves · programs

    Coordinated gene programs, and each target's effect on each.

    Applies to: always

    Calls: cnmf: cNMF prepare, factorize, combine, consensus; scanpy: tl.rank_genes_groups; scanpy: tl.score_genes; scikit-learn: decomposition.NMF; scipy: stats.ranksums; statsmodels: OLS; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    similarity

    Targets alike or opposite, the genes they share and their complexes; sets of targets that act together; the regulators along a programme's axis

    Targets with similar profiles · alike

    Perturbations of one complex or pathway look alike, so an unknown gene can be placed by the known ones it resembles.

    Applies to: 3 or more targets

    Calls: numpy: profiles compared (pearson), each cell against its sample's control cells

    Regulators of one program that act together · sets

    The regulators of one program, clustered by what else they move into sets that act together.

    Applies to: the regulators of one program (C.programs), and their effects on the others

    Two programs the regulators move together or apart · axis

    Two programs that the regulators move in opposite (or the same) directions: an axis of the cell's regulation.

    Applies to: each target's effect on several programs (C.programs)

    conditions

    Effects across conditions, time points, cell types or modes (what: the controls' own response, genes, scores or shares; pair: the two conditions)

    Effects that differ between conditions · conditions

    A perturbation's effect compared between conditions (a stimulus, a time point, a tissue, a cell type).

    Applies to: 2 or more conditions

    Activation against inhibition of one gene · modes

    CRISPRa and CRISPRi of one gene in one library.

    Applies to: CRISPRa and CRISPRi of the same genes in one library

    combinations

    Pairs of perturbations against their singles

    Each pair against its singles · pairs

    Two perturbations together against each alone.

    Applies to: pairs or sets

    Calls: numpy: linalg.lstsq (Norman et al. 2019's fit of a pair on its singles)

    enrichment

    What changed genes mean: gene sets in each target's changed genes from a genes run (de; up and down apart, or method gsea on its ranking) or in a list; the activity of factors and pathways (method ulm with collection collectri or progeny, on a genes run: de); a factor's direct targets

    The gene sets a finding's genes fall in · meaning

    The meaning of a finding's changed genes: the gene sets they over-represent (up and down apart, against the genes the screen detects), or where a ranking enriches (GSEA).

    Applies to: always

    Calls: gseapy: enrich

    A factor's known targets, as its perturbation moves them · direct_targets

    For a perturbed transcription factor, the way its known targets (CollecTRI's regulons) move, against what its regulon and the perturbation predict.

    Applies to: a perturbed regulator (a transcription factor) whose changed genes were measured

    Calls: decoupler: mt.ulm

    reproducibility

    Whether a result holds: the guides of a target agree, the replicates agree, the calls hold with fewer cells

    Agreement between a target's guides · guides

    The agreement of a target's guides, beyond what two control guides share.

    Applies to: 2 or more guides per target

    Calls: numpy: profiles compared (pearson), each cell against its sample's control cells

    Agreement between samples · replicates

    The agreement of the units (mice, donors, captures) on the targets' profiles.

    Applies to: 2 or more samples or lanes

    Calls: numpy: corrcoef (Pearson) of profiles, replicate against replicate

    Each target's effect on the whole profile · fewer_cells

    A target's effect on the cells' whole profile.

    Applies to: always

    Calls: pertpy: tl.DistanceTest; scanpy: pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.pca; statsmodels: stats.multitest.multipletests

    The first look, run by code

    The screen at a glance

    Every target side by side, before any one is followed.

    Applies to: after the first look: every target by every kind of evidence its runs measured

    Calls: pertpy: tl.Distance; scanpy: pp.highly_variable_genes, pp.pca

    The cells the experiment holds, by unit and kind

    This module counts the cells of the experiment per unit (capture, mouse, donor): cells with one target, with two or more, with a control guide, with an ambiguous call, and with no call.

    Applies to: always
